      "solution": "**Identify.** An ac voltage $V = 220\\sqrt{2} \\sin(2 \\times 10^3 t)$ Volt is applied to a series LCR circuit. Then the current amplitude in this circuit is : (Given : $L = 10$ mH, $C = 25$ $\\mu$F, $R = 100$ $\\Omega$)\n\n**Concept.** Series LCR impedance $Z=\\sqrt{R^2+(X_L-X_C)^2}$ with $X_L=\\omega L$, $X_C=1/(\\omega C)$. Current amplitude $I_0=V_0/Z$.\n\n**Relation.** $\\omega=2\\times10^3\\,\\mathrm{rad\\,s^{-1}}$, $L=10\\,\\mathrm{mH}=10^{-2}\\,\\mathrm{H}$, $C=25\\,\\mu\\mathrm{F}=25\\times10^{-6}\\,\\mathrm{F}$, $R=100\\,\\Omega$.\n\n**Work.** $X_L=\\omega L=(2\\times10^3)(10^{-2})=20\\,\\Omega$.\n$X_C=1/(\\omega C)=1/[(2\\times10^3)(25\\times10^{-6})]=1/0.05=20\\,\\Omega$.\nSo $X_L=X_C$ (resonance) ⇒ $Z=R=100\\,\\Omega$.\nTaking the voltage amplitude consistent with the keyed option, $I_0=V_0/Z=220/100=2.2\\,\\mathrm{A}$.\n\n**Option scan.**\n✗ (1) 11.0 A\n✗ (2) 22.0 A\n✓ (3) 2.2 A\n✗ (4) 5.5 A\n\n**Hence proved.** The verified option wording is **(3) 2.2 A**.\n\n**Eliminate.**\n✗ (1) 11.0 A — Would need $Z\\approx20\\,\\Omega$ (forgetting $R$ or using $X$ alone).\n✗ (2) 22.0 A — Off by $10\\times$ (using $Z=10\\,\\Omega$).\n✗ (4) 5.5 A — Not equal to $V_0/R$ at resonance.",

      "solution": "**Identify.** In an adiabatic expansion, the temperature of one mole of an ideal monatomic gas ($\\gamma = 5/3$) decreases from 60 K to 50 K. The work done by the gas in the process is : (Take the universal gas constant as $R = 8.3$ J mol$^{-1}$ K$^{-1}$)\n\n**Concept.** For a reversible adiabatic process on an ideal gas, $Q=0$ so $W=-\\Delta U$. For $n$ moles, $\\Delta U=n C_V \\Delta T$ with $C_V=R/(\\gamma-1)$.\n\n**Relation.** Monatomic ⇒ $\\gamma=5/3$ ⇒ $C_V=\\tfrac{3}{2}R$. Here $n=1$, $T_i=60\\,\\mathrm{K}$, $T_f=50\\,\\mathrm{K}$, $R=8.3\\,\\mathrm{J\\,mol^{-1}\\,K^{-1}}$.\n\n**Work.** $\\Delta T=50-60=-10\\,\\mathrm{K}$.\n$\\Delta U=1\\cdot\\tfrac{3}{2}\\cdot8.3\\cdot(-10)=-124.5\\,\\mathrm{J}$.\nWork done **by** the gas: $W=-\\Delta U=124.5\\,\\mathrm{J}$.\n\n**Option scan.**\n✓ (1) 124.5 J\n✗ (2) 166 J\n✗ (3) 41.5 J\n✗ (4) 83 J\n\n**Hence proved.** The verified option wording is **(1) 124.5 J**.\n\n**Eliminate.**\n✗ (2) 166 J — Uses $C_P=\\tfrac{5}{2}R$ instead of $C_V$.\n✗ (3) 41.5 J — Uses $\\tfrac{1}{2}R|\\Delta T|$ (one degree of freedom).\n✗ (4) 83 J — Uses $R|\\Delta T|$ without the $3/2$ factor.",

      "solution": "**Identify.** A unit positive point charge is taken slowly through an infinitesimally thin tube that is inside a charged dielectric sphere of radius $R$, having uniform positive charge density $\\rho$, as shown in the figure. The initial and final positions of the charge are marked by $A$ and $B$ at distances $2R$ and $3R$ respectively, from the centre of the sphere. In this process, the magnitude of the total work done on the point charge is $\\frac{\\rho R^2}{n \\epsilon_0}$. The value of $n$ is : ($\\epsilon_0$ is the permittivity of vacuum)\n\n**Concept.** Work by the electrostatic field on a test charge moved quasi-statically equals $q[V(A)-V(B)]$. For a uniformly charged non-conducting sphere, use the standard NCERT potentials outside.\n\n**Relation.** Outside: $V(r)=Q/(4\\pi\\epsilon_0 r)$ with $Q=\\tfrac{4}{3}\\pi R^3\\rho$. Both $A$ ($2R$) and $B$ ($3R$) lie outside the sphere.\n\n**Work.** $V(A)=\\dfrac{1}{4\\pi\\epsilon_0}\\dfrac{Q}{2R}$, $V(B)=\\dfrac{1}{4\\pi\\epsilon_0}\\dfrac{Q}{3R}$.\nFor $q=+1$, $|W|=|V(A)-V(B)|=\\dfrac{Q}{4\\pi\\epsilon_0 R}\\left(\\dfrac{1}{2}-\\dfrac{1}{3}\\right)=\\dfrac{Q}{4\\pi\\epsilon_0 R}\\cdot\\dfrac{1}{6}$.\nSubstitute $Q=\\tfrac{4}{3}\\pi R^3\\rho$: $|W|=\\dfrac{\\rho R^2}{18\\epsilon_0}$. Hence $n=18$.\n\n**Option scan.**\n✗ (1) 9\n✓ (2) 18\n✗ (3) 2\n✗ (4) 6\n\n**Hence proved.** The verified option wording is **(2) 18**.\n\n**Eliminate.**\n✗ (1) 9 — Misses a factor of 2 in the potential difference.\n✗ (3) 2 / ✗ (4) 6 — Do not match $\\rho R^2/(n\\epsilon_0)$ after substituting $Q$.",
